"At present, it can be confirmed that, due to an unknown cause and an unknown source of stimulation, Roselle set his sights on Sequence 0."

He didn't explain what Sequence 0 corresponded to. In the Tarot Club, more members knew than didn't. Even those who didn't know could probably guess something.

Sequence 0... The Emperor wanted to become a god? No wonder it's said that he went mad in his later years... Audrey knew what Sequence 0 meant and immediately sighed. Alger reacted similarly.

Cattleya clearly knew what Sequence 0 implied. Deep in thought, she expressed her gratitude and withdrew her gaze.

Sequence 0... Emlyn and Fors mulled over the term, as though they had only just realized that there was a position above Sequence 1 that belonged to true gods!

Derrick looked at Mr. Fool with a puzzled expression. He didn't understand what was wrong with the attempt made by Emperor Roselle, someone with a strong presence in history. In the City of Silver, if anyone had the opportunity, every resident would wish to become a Sequence 0 true god in order to create a more sustainable living environment or lead everyone out of the forsaken land.

Klein didn't elaborate. He leaned back in his chair and surveyed the area.

"You may begin."

With that said, he controlled The World to look at Derrick before giving a deep chuckle.

"The Notary potion formula you wanted has been acquired."

"Thank you, Mr. World. According to our agreement, I will remember this debt and await your request," Derrick said sincerely.

"All right." Klein controlled The World to respond before putting on a show of requesting Mr. Fool to conjure the formula.

Before long, the potion formula was in Little Sun's hands.

Derrick received it with delight and eagerly scanned the contents of the goatskin.

"Sequence 6, Notary. Main ingredients: 1 set of crystallized roots of the Tree of Elders, 5 feathers of a Spirit Pact Bird. Supplementary ingredients: 100 ml of Radiance Spirit Pact Tree's juice, 1 golden-rimmed sunflower, 1 white-rimmed sunflower, 5 drops of Aqua Fern juice."

Without waiting for the others to speak, The World looked at Alger and said, "As you know, I have your Ocean Songster potion formula and main ingredient."

Ah? Audrey and the others were momentarily dumbfounded.

Cattleya couldn't conceal her astonishment as she looked at The World. She clearly remembered that Gehrman Sparrow had hunted Slaughterer Kircheis only a week ago. How had he obtained the Ocean Songster potion formula and main ingredient?

Don't tell me he killed an Ocean Songster? That would be considered a quasi-high-ranking member of the Church of Storms... It's only been a week! Cattleya found it difficult to believe.

Chapter 716: Island and Ruins

Mr. World has also obtained the Ocean Songster potion formula and main ingredient. He's really impressive! Derrick shifted his gaze away from the goatskin in his hand and looked at the gentleman seated at the other end of the long bronze table.

In his mind, The World's strength had reached an entirely new level, one comparable to the weaker members of the six-member council!

Fors was similarly astonished, and her feelings grew more complicated.

She remembered that, when she first joined the Tarot Club, Mr. World had given her the impression of being gloomy and reserved. Apart from that, nothing about him had stood out. In the gatherings that followed, he began to demonstrate extensive experience, broad knowledge, and rather impressive abilities. At a glance, he was a senior Beyonder who had been through many things, second only to Mr. Hanged Man among the Tarot Club members.

This image had already taken shape in Fors's mind, but over the past few months, Miss Magician had repeatedly been surprised by The World.

First, he proved capable of obtaining rather important information. Then, within a short period, he sold many Beyonder characteristics. She involuntarily began to suspect that he was a Beyonder assassin active in the mysterious world. She feared him from the bottom of her heart, but that fear was accompanied by happiness. This meant she could entrust certain requests to Mr. World and have a high chance of seeing them fulfilled.

When the new member, The Hermit, appeared, Fors had even imagined that Mr. World's halo would be overshadowed by this powerful lady. To her surprise, however, he had seized the "limelight" and dazzled everyone with his brilliance.

Obtaining the Ocean Songster potion formula, its corresponding main ingredient, and the Notary potion formula seemed beyond human capabilities!

He really is impressive. I admire this sort of person who appears quiet and ordinary but is actually very capable. Sigh, I wonder when I'll become a Viscount. After this hunting competition, the winner will receive a prize that's likely to be something capable of making a Sanguine stronger... Emlyn White's thoughts quickly returned to himself.

While Audrey marveled in amazement, she felt puzzled about something from before.

Why isn't the impressive Mr. World searching for Emperor Roselle's diary pages? Hmm, he isn't like Ma'am Hermit. He has already been in the Tarot Club for quite some time and doesn't need to observe... It isn't as though he has no requests, or that he must search for Emperor Roselle's diary pages to obtain something from Mr. Fool... Then why isn't he searching for them?

Has he submitted them in private? But why?

Imperceptibly, Audrey sensed that Mr. World and Mr. Fool were connected to a certain degree, but she couldn't confirm it. There were simply too many possibilities.

At that moment, after a few seconds of silence, Alger turned toward the seat of honor.

"Honorable Mr. Fool, I wish to communicate privately with The World."

Here it comes... Klein nodded in anticipation and isolated everyone's senses.

Alger waited two seconds before turning back to look at The World, Gehrman Sparrow.

"I will offer the coordinates of an unknown primitive island in exchange.

"The island not only has extinct Beyonder creatures that can't be found on the Northern or Southern Continent, but also an ancient ruin of unknown age.

"Back then... Phew... Qilangos and I discovered it. At the time, we lacked the strength to delve deeply into it. However, he discovered certain traces and suspected that the ruins concealed something extremely, extremely important. He believed its value was no lower than that of a card created by Emperor Roselle.

"My strength was inferior to Qilangos's, so I didn't venture as deeply as he did. I have no idea what he saw. I could only make guesses based on his remarks."

...Mr. Hanged Man's words contain plenty of information. From the looks of it, he knew Vice Admiral Hurricane Qilangos long ago. They even went on adventures together and discovered the primitive island and ancient ruins. And previously, he was also the one who considered finishing off this pirate admiral while he was away at sea... Mr. Hanged Man isn't an ordinary member of the Church of Storms. His past seems very complicated, and he hides many secrets... Yes, the value of a primitive island isn't small. For a secret organization, it could serve as a foundation to support its members' growth... Klein controlled The World to appear deep in thought as he attempted to obtain more information from The Hanged Man.

When The World neither rejected nor agreed, Alger, who had regained his composure, calmly said, "There are traces of demigod-level Beyonder creatures on the island. To you, that should be something valuable.

"On this point, I can swear in Mr. Fool's name. I guarantee that I'm not lying."

After I obtain the Bizarro Sorcerer potion formula, I'll have to consider how to obtain the corresponding ingredients. At the level of a demigod, money becomes of little use. Trading gives me the greatest chance of obtaining what I need... The location of demigod-level Beyonder creatures is important. Those ruins are also quite interesting... The World didn't hesitate. He chuckled.

"Deal."

Alger didn't immediately receive the Ocean Songster potion formula. Instead, he said, "I have one condition. I wish to accompany you on your first exploration of the primitive island. With Mr. Fool watching over everything, I believe you won't be concerned that I'll attempt anything untoward."

Klein considered it for a moment and made The World nod.

"No problem. I will need companions when venturing into dangerous places. I hope you possess the necessary strength."

Alger wasn't angered by The World's tone. He heaved a sigh of relief.

"That's why I hope your first exploration will take place after I advance. Of course, if I lose control because of the potion, this agreement will naturally become null and void."

"No problem," The World replied without any qualms.

Alger didn't dwell on the topic. After obtaining Mr. Fool's permission, he conjured the details of the primitive island on a piece of goatskin.

It's on the periphery of the Rorsted Sea... It's quite a large island, almost the size of Blue Mountain Island... Klein received the goatskin and quickly scanned it, gaining a general understanding of the primitive island.

He quickly looked up and said, "I'll hand over the potion formula and main ingredient after the gathering ends."

"All right." Having made his choice, Alger no longer had any reservations. Upon hearing those words, waves surged through his heart, but he appeared even more staid on the surface.

After their private conversation ended, Cattleya withdrew her gaze from The World and looked at Fors.

"The Meteorite Crystal has been obtained. 600 pounds for 60 grams."

That's fast... Ma'am Hermit really is efficient! Fors was delighted, but she also felt troubled.

After last week's gathering, she had already obtained the crystallized blood of a Lavos Squid. She had paid 600 pounds for it, leaving her with 230 pounds. Even after adding the 150 pounds she received in royalties this week, she was still 220 pounds short.

I'll borrow from Xio first. She should have that much... After I successfully advance, I'll do my best to earn money as quickly as possible! I have to! Fors silently drew a breath and said, "All right. We will complete the transaction tomorrow at this time."

The Hermit tersely acknowledged her. Without wasting any time, she looked at Audrey.

"The Black-hunting Giant Lizard's spinal fluid has been confirmed. 1,800 pounds. There are no clues regarding the Illusory Chime Tree's fruit for the time being."

"That's already very good, better than I expected. Thank you, Ma'am Hermit." Audrey sincerely thanked her, not minding the price at all.

She had recently realized that there was an advantage to establishing the "Relic Search and Preservation Foundation." She could use her funds in a more discreet and inconspicuous manner, making it difficult for her parents to notice.

After confirming the two transactions, Cattleya surveyed the members as though she were already one of them.

"Any clues regarding the blood of a Mythical Creature?"

Klein immediately controlled The World to chuckle hoarsely.

"Are you referring to a pure Mythical Creature?"

Pure Mythical Creature? Are there impure Mythical Creatures? Audrey and the others were puzzled but intrigued by what they heard.

Cattleya was taken aback. She said, "A pure one, which means the blood of an angel. One drop is enough."

She hadn't introduced it in detail back then because she believed that, for the time being, such a high-level object wouldn't appear in the Tarot Club unless Mr. Fool was willing to help her. And clearly, Mr. Fool didn't need anyone to explain to "Him" what a Mythical Creature was.

"I have certain clues, but I can't guarantee that I'll be able to obtain it. Furthermore, it will take at least another three months." Klein controlled The World to respond.

The other Tarot Club members fell silent. They had never expected The World to have clues regarding an angel's blood!

What can't he accomplish? Audrey, Fors, and the others had their impression of Mr. World refreshed once again.

As for Alger, he suspected that The World intended to ask Mr. Fool's angel for a drop of blood.

After a brief silence, Emlyn, who suddenly realized that his advancement wasn't necessarily as obvious as those of the other members, asked weakly, "Are there any clues regarding my previous commission?"

Fors immediately said, "There are some suspected cases. I will continue investigating."

Burdened with debt, she was highly motivated to work.

At the end of the trading segment, Derrick mentioned his desire to purchase the crystallized roots of the Tree of Elders. As for the feathers of a Spirit Pact Bird, the City of Silver had some in its special warehouse.

In addition, he also sought to purchase the golden-rimmed sunflower and the other supplementary ingredients. They were commonly seen in the outside world, but were unavailable in the Forsaken Land of the Gods.

"The crystallized roots of the Tree of Elders and the supplementary ingredients aren't difficult to obtain." Emlyn's attention was drawn over as he said, "Then what can you offer in exchange?"

When he had provided Miss Justice with the fruit of the Tree of Elders, he had already learned where its crystallized roots could be found.

Derrick thought for a moment and said, "A Sequence 5 Vampire's Beyonder characteristic?"

It was a relatively common monster in the depths of the darkness.

"Sanguine!" Emlyn said through gritted teeth. "There's no hurry. I'll tell you once I've decided what I need."

He didn't want an item obtained through a transaction to overlap with the hunting competition's prize.

"All right." Having already incurred a debt to The World, Derrick was relatively at ease.

After a moment of silence, Klein rapped the edge of the mottled table, signaling the beginning of the free exchange.

Chapter 717: The World and Hero Bandit

Inside the ancient palace standing above the gray fog, Alger wasn't in a hurry to mention the matter concerning Crazy Captain Connors Viktor. He turned his head to look at The Sun.

"Are you still in Afternoon Town?"

Derrick nodded honestly.

"Yes. We spent a very long time establishing a camp. Afterward, we formed small teams and cleared out the remaining monsters one street at a time while searching for any ancient ruins that might still exist. Although the process is slow, it's sufficiently safe."

His answer was extremely detailed, giving the impression that Mr. Hanged Man could ask further questions if he wished to know more.

Audrey listened with interest and raised her hand slightly.

"With all of you staying in Afternoon Town for so long, what do you rely on for food? Is there any Black-Faced Grass nearby?"

She had long since heard from Little Sun that the City of Silver relied on the Black-Faced Grass grown around the city as its source of sustenance.

"We brought some powdered Black-Faced Grass, but our main source of food comes from hunting those monsters. Once the Beyonder characteristic is expelled, a large proportion of them can be skinned, roasted over a fire, and eaten. However, their flesh contains a certain degree of corruption, which can cause psychological problems. We can't consume it continuously and need to leave intervals between meals," Derrick replied seriously to Miss Justice's question.

Audrey recalled the monsters Little Sun had mentioned in the depths of the darkness. Imagining the terrifying creatures in the dark and eerie surroundings of the City of Silver, she couldn't help but pursue the matter further. "Wouldn't that be very disgusting?"

Fors, who had been listening attentively to the other members' conversation, was inspired by the question. Deliberately avoiding Emlyn's gaze, she asked Derrick with keen curiosity, "I remember you mentioning that the monsters in the depths of the darkness include Vampires. Their bodies ooze pus, and they're extremely ugly. Then, after killing Vampires, how would you eat them?"

Upon hearing the question, Emlyn forgot to correct her and explain that Sanguine weren't Vampires. His expression turned livid as an inexplicable, indescribable horror seized him.

Derrick remained silent for two seconds before saying, "Some monsters are indeed disgusting--very disgusting. The Vampires Miss Magician mentioned are one such example, but we have no choice. As long as something is edible, we will eat it."

His voice gradually grew heavy, as though he had once again profoundly felt the tragedy of the curse enveloping the City of Silver.

The palace that resembled a giant's residence once again fell silent. Even Emlyn, who had wanted to retort, said nothing. He merely curled his lips and drew back his arm.

A few seconds later, The World broke the silence with a chuckle.

"Let's return to the sea. Certain matters have arisen that require your attention. After a storm, Crazy Captain Connors Viktor's Single-eyed Skull drifted into a nearby harbor. Its masts were broken, and it was covered in charred marks. The entire crew, including Connors Viktor, was dead. Not a single person survived."

Justice and the others didn't know who Crazy Captain was. They weren't particularly interested in the news, apart from being curious about what had caused this devastating maritime tragedy.

Alger, on the other hand, had thoughts entirely different from theirs.

There must be an enormous secret involved if The World is paying attention to Crazy Captain's matter! No wonder His Eminence Kottman issued such an important order for the Mandated Punishers and captains to investigate the situation! Alger's eyes shifted slightly as he pondered before saying, "In Bayam, the Church of Storms is actively investigating the people and matters connected to Crazy Captain Connors Viktor."

Heh, there's no need for me to commission him... Klein chuckled inwardly and had The World ask directly, "What clues does the Church of Storms possess?"

"I have no idea. I'll do my best to learn about the situation." Alger frankly shook his head.

He believed that The World understood his implication: although he didn't know the exact details, he would sound out how much the Church knew.

Audrey, who was listening out of curiosity, realized that Crazy Captain's matter wasn't simple. Therefore, she asked probingly, "Mr. World, isn't this common infighting among pirates?"

Klein was just about to give Miss Justice a perfunctory answer without divulging anything further when a problem suddenly occurred to him.

Behind the Great Smog of Backlund and those disappearances, there might be a particular faction of the royal family, as well as certain military personnel. And both emotionally and by virtue of her position, Miss Justice leans toward the nobility and royal family. If the day comes when I target those bastards who deserve to die, she might be unable to accept it if I commission her. She might even find herself caught in a dilemma...

Therefore, I should begin instilling in her the impression that the royal family and military contain plenty of villains. I can subtly shift the direction of her sympathies and position. For that, I can accept the risk of leaking some information about The World... Klein pondered for a moment before having The World say in a relaxed but mocking tone, "Crazy Captain is connected to the disappearance of the colonial slaves Mr. Hanged Man was previously investigating.

"The Demoness Sect hands the people they deceive or abduct to Vice Admiral Ailment Tracy, who ships them to the eastern coast of the Northern Continent. Crazy Captain Connors Viktor then completes the final leg. This pirate is intricately connected to many human traffickers and slave merchants in Loen.

"Back when I informed Mr. Hanged Man of the news in January, someone witnessed Baelen, who was involved in the disappearances, together with an MI9 member loyal to the royal family.

"Furthermore, Capim, Backlund's largest human trafficker, had guards from the Arbiter pathway.

"With all these matters taken together, do you still think Crazy Captain's death was simply the result of infighting among pirates?

"Heh heh, I've always wondered why the person behind all this needs so many slaves."

The connections between the clues were clearly laid out before Audrey, Alger, and the others, causing them to suddenly realize many things.

Mr. World left Backlund and went to sea in search of clues. He's been investigating the truth behind the Great Smog and Prince Edessak's death all along! According to the information he provided, the true murderer clearly hasn't been punished. He's still hiding within the royal family, and part of the military is working for him. How despicable! Such a person should be cast into hell! Hmm... How does Mr. World know that Capim's guards were Beyonders of the Arbiter pathway? Did he fight them? Audrey's thoughts raced as she suddenly made a connection.

Capim was killed by Hero Bandit Black Emperor. The latter clearly knew which pathway Capim's guards belonged to.

Hero Bandit Black Emperor is Mr. Fool's Blessed.

Mr. World is suspected of having a certain connection to Mr. Fool. He has never requested Emperor Roselle's diary pages.

Therefore, Mr. World is Hero Bandit Black Emperor, and he's also Mr. Fool's Blessed?

He doesn't hand in Emperor Roselle's diary pages because he gives them to Mr. Fool in private? Some of his previous actions were meant to conceal his identity as a Blessed? Is this a test Mr. Fool gave him? It's truly difficult to connect Mr. World with Hero Bandit Black Emperor. The latter feels like a hero... Audrey felt as though she had stumbled upon the truth, and she began wondering what kind of person Mr. World, who was also Hero Bandit Black Emperor, was in real life.

Alger and Cattleya instantly understood why Gehrman Sparrow had attacked Tracy.

This Blessed was searching for the truth behind the Great Smog of Backlund!

And the truth concealed behind it was something Mr. Fool was interested in!

Fors and Emlyn, who lived in Backlund, also vaguely sensed something when The World mentioned Capim. After all, the legend of Hero Bandit Black Emperor had been rather prominent over the past half year. He had become an important character in many run-of-the-mill novels. Even Fors herself had considered making him the protagonist of a romance between a hero bandit and a young lady of the royal family.

They finally understood why The World had noticed in time that something was brewing in Backlund and that a tragedy was about to occur. It was because this gentleman had been following the relevant clues all along.

From the looks of it, the matter in Backlund is far from over. Another incident will likely occur in the future... I really want to flee this place immediately, but Xio definitely wouldn't agree. I have no way to explain it to her... The thought of fleeing Backlund suddenly arose in Fors's mind.

Emlyn wasn't particularly afraid. From his perspective, the Sanguine's important figures were all in Backlund and would definitely be able to provide a measure of protection if a disaster truly occurred.

After two seconds of silence, Alger said solemnly, "I will do my best to investigate this matter."

He had already sensed that a rather terrifying storm lay hidden behind the truth of Crazy Captain's death. As such, he felt somewhat apprehensive and afraid, but he also trembled with excitement at being involved in a matter of the utmost importance to both the Southern and Northern Continents.

Upon hearing this, Cattleya nodded.

"I will also seek out the relevant information.

"If you can provide more clues, I might be able to help."

Klein pondered for a moment before having The World state frankly, "On the night Crazy Captain died, a High-Sequence Beyonder suspected of belonging to the Black Emperor pathway appeared aboard his ship. However, this demigod managed to escape before Jahn Kottman arrived."

Black Emperor pathway, demigod... Alger and Cattleya mulled over those phrases as they considered how to approach the matter.

Audrey and the others were astonished that the Tarot Club had gone from discussing demigods to becoming directly involved in matters concerning them. The level of the cases they were involved in was truly increasing. As for Derrick, he didn't understand any of it.

With Crazy Captain's matter concluded, Cattleya glanced at The World before deliberately sharing her own piece of news.

"Another incident occurred at sea. Last week, the crazy adventurer Gehrman Sparrow hunted Slaughterer Kircheis, the second mate of the King of Immortality. He was a Sequence 5 Beyonder."

"How impressive..." Fors, who was only a Sequence 8, sighed from the bottom of her heart. She knew very well what it meant to be a Sequence 5.

"Yes, he truly is a legendary adventurer," Audrey echoed.

Emlyn's mouth fell open, then closed again. He felt that he was still some distance from that level.

Alger said without displaying any unease, "It is precisely because of this that investigating Gehrman Sparrow's origins has become an important mission for the various major organizations."

Are you implying that the Church of Storms has ordered you to investigate me? Klein instantly read between the lines and had The World chuckle.

"I wonder where I can claim this commission. I'd like to exchange some unimportant information for money. I do have some understanding of Gehrman Sparrow."

Oh, Mr. World is hinting that he doesn't mind if I exchange information about Gehrman Sparrow that appears important but is actually meaningless for rewards and trust? The Hanged Man nodded in understanding.

The free exchange continued for a while. After Derrick finished learning this week's list of ancient Hermes terms, the gathering came to an end.

After watching the bowing Miss Justice and the others depart, Klein cast his gaze upon Groselle's Travels.

Chapter 718: Characters in the Book

As he wasn't overly exhausted, Klein rubbed his temples and beckoned a small metal bottle over from the junk pile.

Inside it was a tiny tube of blood that he had gone to great lengths to draw from his vein and had long since brought above the gray fog. It was waiting for the opportunity when Klein would use his Spirit Body to enter Groselle's Travels and explore the book's world.

After opening the cap, Klein didn't immediately smear the blood over the book's dark brown cover. Instead, he summoned all the items from the junk pile that he had brought into the mysterious space before the Tarot Gathering and spread them out before him.

Considering how conspicuous the Black Emperor's form was and how little he knew about the situation in the book's world, Klein decided against bringing the Card of Blasphemy. Instead, he used Azik's copper whistle to strengthen his Spirit Body, preventing some unknown power from instantly killing him before he could return above the gray fog.

As the ancient and exquisite copper whistle fused with his body, Klein's Spirit Body swelled and became more corporeal.

Two pitch-black flames flared in his eye sockets as though they possessed lives of their own.

With the aid of Cogitation, he adjusted his spirituality and restrained the iciness stemming from the aspect of Death within his body. Soon, his eyes returned to normal.

It was like an evil spirit assuming an ordinary appearance to lure prey close.

Immediately afterward, Klein put on Creeping Hunger and held Death Knell while concealing the Nightmare Beyonder characteristic inside his body. He had prepared the latter so he could search for abnormalities by exploring the dreams of living beings in the book's world.

After completing all his preparations, he poured out a few drops of blood and smeared them over the cover of Groselle's Travels.

After a brief wait, his vision blurred, as though countless translucent objects were concealed within it. Then, it cleared--blue skies, white clouds, grayish-brown city walls, and passersby appeared before him.

This is no longer the frozen land from before, but a city that appears perfectly normal on the surface... Klein stood beside the dirt road and observed the residents of the book's world. He discovered that most of them wore linen shirts, short brown coats, and loose, dark-colored trousers. Their overall style resembled that of the Loen Kingdom several centuries ago.

He looked down at the style of his tailcoat, starched shirt, and damaged dark red bow tie and silently changed them all. Instantly, he looked no different from the people around him.

He then walked toward the city gates, preparing to enter.

At that moment, a soldier in a leather vest stopped him at the gate.

"Entrance fee! One liddle."

Do I look rich? I don't even know what a liddle is... Klein lampooned inwardly in amusement. He then "communicated" with the soldier on a spiritual level, successfully shifting the man's attention to a merchant caravan behind him.

As a quasi-wraith capable of possessing and controlling anyone, exerting a psychological influence on others was nothing extraordinary. It wasn't a particularly powerful ability, but it was extremely useful against ordinary people.

After entering the city, Klein walked through the streets in an apparently relaxed manner while inwardly remaining vigilant. He noticed that public hygiene was slightly better than Backlund's had been a few years ago. The city appeared to have a mature sewage system, preventing people from dumping waste and trash indiscriminately.

I can't tell that this is an illusory world inside a book at all. Everyone has Spirit Body Threads... Klein continued forward while observing his surroundings. Suddenly, he noticed a stone building more than ten meters tall beside him. It had only two stories, and the top of its doorway stood about four meters above the ground.

There was a sign beside the building. It was written in a language entirely different from any in the outside world, yet Klein instantly understood its meaning: "Pessote Blacksmith Guild."

There's a blacksmith guild. This place truly hasn't entered the Age of Steam... Just as Klein was sighing with emotion, he saw the door creak open and a giant with four long limbs walk out!

The giant's skin was grayish-blue, and his head bore the characteristic single vertical eye. He carried a huge, heavy sledgehammer in one hand and crossed the street with his lips curled.

The humans passing him displayed no fear, as though this were a common sight.

They even greeted the giant.

"Good afternoon, Groselle!"

Groselle... Klein, who had considerable difficulty distinguishing one giant from another, felt his pupils contract. Only then did he realize why the giant looked familiar!

He was just about to give chase when he saw that the giant had turned onto another street and vanished from sight.

Klein stood where he was, silently observing the intersection as several conjectures occurred to him.

There's another Groselle in the book's world?

No, the travels ended with Groselle dying in battle in the Nation of Frost...

Is this another story?

Filled with all kinds of questions, Klein didn't rush to find Groselle. He turned and entered a bar beside the street.

Such establishments were often the places where the most varied and disorderly information in a city gathered. It would help him quickly grasp the overall situation.

The bar was dimly lit and poorly ventilated, making the air rather turbid. At that moment, there weren't many patrons drinking. Most were gathered around the bar, chatting merrily with one another or with the bartender.

Klein slowly walked over, only for his gaze to freeze.

Beside the bar, he saw a man in a pointed black hat and an asymmetrical coat. The man was rather handsome, with flaxen hair, deep brown eyes, a high nose, and thin lips. He was none other than Viscount Mobet Zoroast of the Solomon Empire!

Upon seeing him, Klein recalled the scene of the Dream Stealer rapidly aging and collapsing to the ground, struggling to crawl toward Elven Songster Siatas before grabbing her hand.

Everything remained as vivid as if it had happened only yesterday, yet Mobet had once again appeared before his eyes.

Klein's expression grew solemn as he walked over and sat beside Mobet.

He didn't say a word, knowing that Mobet was the sort to start a conversation on his own.

"Outsider, is this your first time in Pessote? I swear I've never met you before." Mobet put down a cup of distilled liquor and turned his head toward him.

"I come from the Nation of Frost." Klein casually fabricated a story.

Mobet immediately burst into loud laughter.

"You're a funny fellow. This is the Nation of Frost. Of course, that was many years ago.

"Ever since the King of the North was killed by a group of adventurers--no, heroes--this place has no longer been plagued by ice and snow. Everyone believes it should be called the Nation of Neverwinter."

Klein remained silent without responding.

"Why so serious? You look like you have troubles of your own." Mobet rapped on the wooden bar with apparent sympathy. He downed a mouthful of spirits and said, "Let me tell you, a man should never get married. It's the beginning of suffering! Do you know? Whenever she gets even a little worked up, she beats me. When she's happy, she beats me. The same happens when she's embarrassed or angry! In any case, she beats me for every reason imaginable! As of this very moment, I've decided never to return home again!"

Does this mean you married Siatas? Klein remained silent for two seconds as he studied Mobet's face. He discovered no signs of bruising or swelling, which meant that the Elven Songster understood that a man needed to maintain appearances.

He asked with a sigh, "Then why did you marry her?"

Mobet was taken aback before smiling ruefully.

"I came here with a merchant caravan. When I first saw her, she was simply so beautiful. Her singing was moving and seemed to conceal an indescribable sorrow. Heh, I'm now as afraid of her as I was infatuated with her back then! I definitely won't go home. Hey, why do you look a little sad? You don't need to feel sorry for me. I'm already free!"

At that moment, the door to the bar suddenly opened, and a beautiful female voice shouted, "Mobet, come out here!

"I'm counting down from ten. If you don't come home, never come back!

"Ten, nine..."

Mobet immediately leaped to his feet and ran toward the door. As he ran, he muttered, "I know you don't have any patience. You always jump straight to two after reaching eight!"

Klein turned and saw Siatas's figure, but he no longer had any intention of communicating with her.

The real Mobet and Siatas were already dead. The two living in the book's world were merely characters.

Klein stood and left the bar, making his way to a nearby deserted alley. He planned to confirm what kind of spirit world existed here.

He quickly outlined spherical lights in his mind as his thoughts gradually emptied and his body and mind grew tranquil.

As his spirituality spread outward bit by bit, several indescribable illusory figures appeared around him. However, the seven lustrous lights of different colors that contained endless knowledge were absent from the sky above.

The seven lights don't exist here... There are very few spirit world creatures as well... This truly is a false spirit world created by the book... Klein took a step forward and found that the colors in his vision had become extremely saturated, overlapping in clearly distinct layers.

He didn't rush to explore the spirit world. He left it and began strolling through Pessote, looking in shop windows and chatting with others.

Before long, he found Groselle's residence.

The giant ran a blacksmith shop and was taking an afternoon nap on an enormous bed on the second floor.

Klein passed directly through the wall and arrived beside Groselle.

He observed the giant for a few seconds, then took the Nightmare Beyonder characteristic out of his Spirit Body. With some difficulty, he used one of the powers it naturally possessed.

Deep, serene darkness rapidly spread, instantly enveloping Klein and Groselle. In his Spirit Body state, Klein directly saw an irregular, hazy sphere of light.

His spirituality immediately extended toward it and made contact with the sphere.

All kinds of fragmented scenes flashed around him before settling on a forest of towering but withered trees. On the other side of the forest stood a mountain with steep cliffs. At its summit was a magnificent palace.

The palace was enormous and opulent, clearly not built for humans. It seemed to have emerged from a myth, and the light of dusk scattered above it appeared frozen in place.

Klein had seen this palace before. It was the Giant King's Court that had appeared in the dream world of the battlefield of the gods!

However, the angle was completely different from before. He was behind the king's court!

Chapter 719: Dream Tour

Klein turned his gaze toward the forest whose dense canopy scarcely allowed any light through, gaining a rough idea of where he was.

This was the Waning Forest that Groselle had guarded while at the Giant King's Court.

The trees in the forest stood dozens of meters tall, and each trunk was so thick that several giants would have been needed to encircle it. However, the bark was mottled everywhere with signs of decay. Most of the leaves and branches had withered and drooped, intertwining like a dark cloud suspended in midair.

Groselle and several similar-looking giants guarded the forest's perimeter. Some carried axes, while others carried broadswords, and all were fully focused on defending the area.

According to Groselle, the bodies of Giant King Aurmir's parents are buried in the Waning Forest. Apart from this ancient god, no one else is allowed to enter, including the guards... Yes, Giant King Aurmir's parents should be what are known as primordial giants, the maddest, cruelest, and most ferocious kind. Perhaps... Eh, why would Groselle have such a dream? As Klein pondered, he suddenly sensed that something was amiss.

According to the casual conversations he had held in Pessote, the present Groselle was a native giant who had nothing to do with the Giant King's Court.

Therefore, having such a dream appeared rather abnormal!

Based on the Psychology Alchemists' theory that Ma'am Daly and Miss Justice discussed before, perhaps when the book's world creates a character, it uses or copies the original person's subconscious or collective subconscious. It then makes minor alterations, causing the consciousness to conform to the required setting. Therefore, Groselle's dream is influenced by his subconscious and recreates his life in the Giant King's Court... If that's the case, one can only say that this book is a cruel author... The moment Klein thought of this, an idea suddenly occurred to him. He felt that this was an opportunity to gather relevant information about the Giant King's Court.

He had previously planned to learn about such matters directly from Groselle. However, to keep his promise, the Giant Guardian had died in the battle against the King of the North, Ulyssan. After his Spirit Body left the book's world, it had rapidly dissipated without giving Klein any chance to communicate with him. Now, he finally had another method: exploring Groselle's dream.

Some parts were bound to be absurd or exaggerated, but the remaining content had to be a true reflection of reality. As long as he studied it carefully, distinguishing between the two wasn't impossible.

Groselle never entered the Waning Forest, so the scenes within must come from his imagination. There's no need to explore it... Klein slowly turned his gaze toward the mountain where the king's court stood.

It wasn't very high, which meant that the Waning Forest was located on a mountain quite close to the king's court. There was probably a direct route from here to the ancient god's residence.

Klein didn't waste time searching. He walked straight up to Groselle, acting as though they knew each other well, and asked in a relaxed tone, "How do I return to the king's court?"

He knew that Groselle was an honest giant, and he would only be more honest in his dream.

Groselle raised a hand and scratched the back of his head. Looking down in confusion, he smiled and said, "Don't you just take the Barren Tunnel?"

He pointed ahead and added, "You'll see it once you go around that boulder."

"Thank you." Klein sighed and bowed.

As he watched Klein leave, Groselle scratched the back of his head again and muttered in bewilderment, "Who is he? Why does he seem so familiar..."

After circling a boulder that jutted from the mountainside, Klein's view suddenly opened up. An enormous cave at least thirty meters tall appeared before him.

A stone stele stood outside the cave. Engraved upon it were a single vertical eye, a high-bridged nose, and thick lips. It looked as though a giant's head had been flattened, leaving only its frontal features visible.

Just as Klein approached, the mouth on the stele opened.

"Why are you returning to the king's court ahead of schedule?"

"By His Majesty's orders," Klein answered calmly. After all, the intelligence of every living being in this dream was equivalent to that of the dream's owner, Groselle.

The stele's lips opened and closed, producing a droning sound.

"Please answer a question. Otherwise, you shall not pass."

...If only I'd brought Arrodes along. It would be quite amusing to see what happened... Klein lampooned inwardly as he calmly nodded.

"All right."

The stele's lips remained closed for three seconds before opening again.

"If your wife, your daughter, and a woman you desire ask you to judge which of them is the most beautiful, whom would you choose?"

This is completely different from the magic mirror's style... Klein's lips twitched as his mind raced. After nearly ten seconds, he answered, "My intelligence is insufficient to decide this matter. I would appoint someone more intelligent than me to provide the answer."

How could I answer a question that might get me killed? Gritting his teeth, he added inwardly.

"Who is this more intelligent person?" The giant's face on the stele froze for several seconds.

Klein answered solemnly, "Our king, of course."

The stele was struck speechless. It took quite some time before it said, "All right, I'll consider that an answer to the question. You may pass."

Klein immediately walked past the strange stele and entered the cave.

The cave's floor was paved with large, weathered stone slabs. Murals covered the walls and ceiling, telling stories of giants and dragons battling demonic wolves, mutants, devils, and phoenixes. The style was crude and the colors dark, but the scenes were exceptionally vivid.

Klein walked forward while examining the murals. He discovered patches of withered weeds and all kinds of coarse gravel in the gaps between the stone slabs and at the base of the murals.

The deeper he ventured, the more apparent the lack of water and decline of life became.

After walking for an unknown length of time, Klein saw an enormous grayish-blue door standing open. On either side stood a giant four to five meters tall.

The giants guarding this place differed from Groselle and the others. They wore sturdy, beautifully made iron-black armor and solid, exquisite helmets, making them resemble two enormous statues.

They didn't stop Klein, allowing him to pass through the doorway and enter the hall beyond.

The hall wasn't particularly spacious. Its far end was clearly visible, and it could probably accommodate only five or six giants.

As Klein observed his surroundings, he suddenly came to a halt. Then, as though lifted by an invisible hand, the hall rapidly rose.

He staggered slightly before regaining his footing. All he could see were grayish-black walls flashing past and sweeping downward.

About ten seconds later, there was a thud, and the hall stopped ascending.

At that moment, beyond the door was no longer the cave tunnel but a magnificent palace supported by stone columns.

Klein briskly left the original hall and surveyed his surroundings with keen interest.

This is the Giant King's Court's "elevator"? This appears to be where the guards live. Outside is a long table taller than a human, surrounded by enormous chairs. There are rooms on both sides, and the beds inside are arranged in neat rows... Klein's gaze swept across the various objects in the hall before stopping on a mural.

The mural's central figure was a giant clad in full-body silver armor. With nothing nearby to provide scale, Klein couldn't determine exactly how tall he was.

The giant stood at the edge of a cliff, holding a sword that pointed diagonally upward. A brilliant halo radiated from his body like a rising sun illuminating his surroundings.

Many giants knelt around him as though praying to or worshiping him while awaiting a bestowment.

The Giant King's son, the God of Dawn Badheil? Klein studied the central figure's face and saw that it was concealed behind a mask. Only a dawn-like radiance shone from his eyes.

It's very similar to the statue of the God of Combat in Backlund's underground ruins. His face is likewise completely hidden behind a mask... Heh, Queen Mystic previously said that the God of Combat is a giant who has lived since ancient times. That's why the Great Twilight Hall, the Church's headquarters, resembles the Giant King's Court... Could he be the Giant King's son? Did the God of Dawn survive the destruction of the king's court and, at some point, regain the authority once wielded by "His" father? Klein made a bold conjecture, but he had no evidence or clues to support it.

Applying the principle of correspondence, he looked at the wall opposite the mural. There was another mural there, but its central figure was no longer the God of Dawn Badheil. Instead, it depicted a female giant wearing a leather vest and a long skirt.

The female giant was shown in profile. Her facial contours were gentle, and her single vertical eye was focused on the area below. Long, dark brown hair fell all the way down her back.

Her right hand was open, holding wheat, fruit, and similar items. Golden fields, clear lakes, fruit-laden trees, and colorful mushrooms surrounded her.

Giant Queen, Goddess of Harvest Omebella? Klein looked around but saw no mural representing Giant King Aurmir.

There's no depiction of the ancient god because this is the residence of guards stationed in a remote area? Then, going outside should lead to the interior of the Giant King's Court... Klein cautiously walked to the door. Using the same method he had employed in the dream world of the battlefield of the gods, he activated Creeping Hunger and used a Zombie's strength to open it.

However, beyond the door was not the palace frozen in dusk that he had imagined. Instead, there was a gray, hazy world. It appeared to be a cliff overlooking a bottomless abyss.

Based on Miss Justice's previous experience, this is likely the boundary of the dream. The only way forward is to descend into Groselle's subconscious. Eventually, I'll reach the sea of collective subconscious... Miss Justice discovered a mind dragon in the sea of collective subconscious formed by the humans around her. Then, in this book's world, which was created by the Dragon of Imagination, what might its sea of collective subconscious contain? Klein's thoughts raced as he conjured a staircase leading down into the hazy world.

The staircase didn't descend in a straight line. Instead, it spiraled deep into the gray haze. Its end was nowhere to be seen, nor could any details of the mind world be discerned.

Chapter 720: Philosopher

After silently observing it for several seconds, Klein stepped onto the staircase and carefully began descending.

The surrounding light gradually dimmed, and a gray, hazy radiance enveloped the deathly silent world. As Klein continued down the stairs, he increasingly felt as though he had been locked inside a dark, silent room. Gradually, he could hear the blood rushing through his veins and the beating of his heart.

The latter quickened as uncontrollable panic and terror assailed him. Klein focused his mind and visualized layers of spherical lights, stabilizing his emotions and regaining his composure.

Beside him, a grayish-white cliff stood coldly, representing the domain of Groselle's consciousness. It was silent as though dead, but points of light occasionally shimmered within the surrounding grayness.

Klein focused his gaze and saw that one point of light contained the image of a giant tearing a human apart and stuffing the pieces into his mouth, as well as a terrified Groselle. At the time, the latter was less than three meters tall and clearly still an adolescent.

The point of light flashed past, replaced by the frozen dusk that blanketed the mountain peak. Time here seemed to slow to a crawl.

Klein was just about to search Groselle's subconscious for valuable information when he suddenly heard a sound resembling the panting of beasts.

With a whoosh, an enormous palm emerged from the surrounding haze. Its skin was grayish-blue and covered in signs of decay, with conspicuous yellowish-green liquid oozing from it as it rapidly grabbed for Klein's ankle.

Amid the panting, similar palms rose one after another from below the staircase, as though attempting to drag Klein's Spirit Body by force into the darkest and most unfathomable region of the mind world.

In an instant, the rotting palms formed a dense horde. They struggled upward while emitting dreadful, hair-raising pants, causing Klein to instinctively leap three steps higher.

However, the countless palms belonging to giant corpses didn't stop. They writhed along the staircase and surged upward, covering every inch of space.

Klein was about to reach out with his right hand, draw Death Knell, and use Purifying Bullets in conjunction with its Slaughter ability to eliminate the countless monsters when two questions suddenly flashed through his mind.

Where did these palms come from? Why would they exist in Groselle's subconscious?

The moment these thoughts arose, his spiritual perception was triggered. Klein came to a realization and immediately abandoned the idea of using Death Knell. He calmed his breathing and visualized the spherical lights.

The enormous, rotting palms seized this opportunity to reach his feet and grab his calves and ankles!

At that moment, they silently vanished as though they had never appeared.

Indeed, this is an illusion created by Groselle's subconscious. Here, minds don't merely confront each other; they influence each other as well. Without the corresponding Beyonder powers, the deeper one goes, the easier it is to suffer an emotional collapse. The other party's subconscious will gradually invade, severely contaminating one's Beyonder powers. As a result, one will become a mental patient who can never recover their rationality, and this might very well lead to losing control... This differs from communicating with spirits. Merely maintaining lucidity and reason can't prevent corruption, because one is already inside the target's Beyonder powers... Klein muttered inwardly, having gained a degree of understanding.

He hesitated for several seconds before turning around and climbing back up the stairs. He abandoned the attempt to venture deep into Groselle's mind world because he lacked the Beyonder powers needed to placate his own mind. Insisting on descending would be tantamount to suicide.

I'll explore it again after I find a mystical item suited to this purpose. Having made up his mind, Klein quickened his pace. Finally, with a leap, he returned to Groselle's dreamscape and the guards' quarters in the Giant King's Court.

He already felt exhausted, so he immediately left the dream. Passing through the walls of Groselle's smithy, he emerged outside and began observing the wonders of the book's world.

I've already met Groselle, Mobet, and Siatas. During casual conversations with others, I also heard about the devout priest Snowman and the philosopher Frunziar. However, there is no Anderson Hood, Edwina Edwards, Danitz, or Gehrman Sparrow... Therefore, do only the dead gain entirely new characters in the book? Or is it that only those who stayed here for an extended period, fully expressing themselves through their daily lives and adventures, have their subconscious copied? As Klein strolled down a street illuminated by the setting sun, he pondered a question he found crucial.

If the former theory was correct, with the dead being "revived" as new characters, Klein had nothing to worry about. However, if it was the latter, he needed to consider exploring the book less frequently and strictly limiting the duration of each visit.

There's no way to determine the truth for now. I'll handle the situation under the assumption that it's the latter. One can never be too cautious... Klein quickly made up his mind and was about to return above the gray fog.

At that moment, he spotted another familiar figure.

Black-haired and blue-eyed, Frunziar sat on a long wooden bench beside the street. He stared blankly at the sky, which appeared to be ablaze, as though lost in thought.

Recalling that he still had the Loen soldier's urn of ashes and planned to return it to the Church of Storms' cemetery in Backlund, Klein sighed inwardly, walked over, and sat beside him. As though engaging in casual conversation, he asked, "What are you thinking about?"

"I'm wondering who I am, where I came from, and how I can return..." Frunziar said dreamily without looking away.

Without waiting for Klein to ask another question, he shook his head and chuckled.

"I always feel that I don't belong here, that I'm not truly myself right now, and that somewhere is waiting for me to return.

"They always mock me for pondering such pointless questions, so they gave me the title of 'philosopher'..."

As he spoke, he looked at the setting sun and once again fell silent, entranced.

Klein said nothing. He sat there quietly, accompanying Frunziar as he watched the sunset. Finally, he silently vanished.

Frunziar didn't notice that the person beside him had already left. He remained seated and motionless like a marble statue, gazing into the distance.

After replenishing the ship's supplies, Alger Wilson ordered the Blue Avenger to leave the Resistance's private harbor. It wouldn't remain in the Rorsted Archipelago for long.

He had to return to Pasu Island and make his report by the appointed time.

At that moment, he stood in the captain's cabin, watching expectantly as items filled with spirituality and a burgeoning flame formed an illusory door.

It was the door of sacrifice, as well as the door of bestowment!

Amid an illusory creak, the mysterious door slowly opened, revealing limitless depth and darkness within.

Light shot out from inside before immediately converging. Once everything had settled, two items had appeared on the altar at some unknown point. As for the door covered in all kinds of strange symbols, it was gone.

Alger managed to compose himself and solemnly thanked Mr. Fool. He completed the ritual according to procedure before reaching out to pick up the two items.

One was a neatly folded piece of paper, while the other was a translucent jellyfish containing azure seawater.

Alger examined the latter and discovered that whirlwinds occasionally formed inside it, interspersed with flashes of lightning. From time to time, he could also hear a distant, stirring voice emanating from within.

This voice sounds like a woman's... Judging by this, the characteristic's former owner was a woman. Alger couldn't help sighing in relief. This meant that the person who had been killed wasn't some quasi-high-ranking member of the Church of Storms.

After all, the Church of Storms had no female high-ranking members, quasi or otherwise!

After putting away the Ocean Songster Beyonder characteristic, Alger unfolded the piece of paper. His gaze swept past the main ingredients, quickly passed over the supplementary ingredients, and finally stopped on the ritual.

To him, the main ingredients were unimportant now that he possessed a Beyonder characteristic. He could examine them later. As for the supplementary ingredients, they were fairly easy to find and didn't require much attention. The ritual was what mattered most.

Drink the potion inside an Obninsk's belly... Alger silently read the ritual's contents as the corresponding information rapidly surfaced in his mind.

Obninsks were ancient sea monsters capable of swallowing a sailboat whole. Each had an enormous, distorted body with as many as three heads and numerous tentacles tangled together. They were the protagonists of many maritime legends.

Most of these sea monsters had been tamed by the Church of Storms and remained within fixed territories. However, it was unknown whether they possessed intelligence approaching that of humans.

No wonder the Church controls Obninsks rather than other sea monsters... No wonder there are so many Beyonders of the Sailor pathway among pirates, yet very few reach Sequence 5. They either inherited the characteristic directly or are subordinates of the King of the Five Seas or Queen Mystic... Then, where can I find an Obninsk that doesn't belong to the Church... Alger frowned slightly as he contemplated how to advance without involving the Church of Storms.

His first thought was to contact Queen Mystic Bernadette through Admiral of Stars Cattleya. Through her, he could find an Obninsk that didn't belong to the Church of Storms. His second thought was that doing so would expose him to danger. After all, the relevant Obninsk was likely a servant of Queen Mystic and would report everything to its mistress.

Yes, that will be my last resort if there are no other options. Alger's thoughts raced, and another idea quickly occurred to him.

He could pray to Mr. Fool!

This reawakened existence secretly wielded the former authority of Sea God Kalvetua. "He" could command undersea creatures, allowing "Him" to know where to find Obninsks unaffiliated with any faction!

There's no need to rush. If I advance now, I won't be able to conceal it from the others because I lack sufficient control over the dissipation of spirituality. I can pray after making my report and leaving Pasu Island... Alger calmed down and memorized the Ocean Songster formula. Then, he moved the paper to the tip of the candle flame.

As he watched the flame consume the formula with increasing speed, Alger's gaze deepened.

After eliminating the remaining traces, his gaze landed on a nautical chart and fixed upon a particular location.

Bansy!

Alger planned to stop by Bansy while on his way to Pasu Island. He wanted to learn the harbor's present condition.

He had already shared the idea with his sailors, and no one objected. They were equally curious as to why Bansy Harbor had suddenly been destroyed and what it had become.

Chapter 721: Klein's Guidance

On the Future, Frank Lee rolled up his sleeves and twirled his fountain pen between his fingers as he wrote with a smile.

"My dear friend, Gehrman Sparrow, I have good news for you. I have successfully cultivated a new variety of mushroom using the flesh and blood of a Rose Bishop. As long as it has fish, it will keep growing. We'll no longer have to worry about not being able to eat mushrooms on long voyages. Furthermore, I crossbred it with beef, so it tastes excellent!

"Its only flaw is that I haven't found a way to make it catch fish by itself. External help is needed, but I don't think that's much of a problem. After all, based on what Nina said, it wouldn't corrupt the ocean. Well, let's just pretend she's right.

"I've mailed you some dried mushrooms. As long as you give them water and fish, they will immediately return to normal and reproduce on their own. I hope you will like my gift..."

After writing at length, Frank finally folded the letter and placed it in an envelope. He stuffed three dried mushrooms into it before gluing it shut.

After doing all of this, he took out the note Klein had given him, followed its instructions, and solemnly began preparing the ritual needed to summon the messenger.

This wasn't complicated for Frank, so it didn't take long for him to set up the altar and create a wall of spirituality.

Finally, he solemnly placed a Loen gold coin before the candle.

He lit the candle, softly chanted the incantation, and stared at the flame. He watched it swell as a headless woman walked out with four heads in hand.

Frank jumped in fright, then stared intently at Reinette Tinekerr's four identical, beautiful, blonde-haired, red-eyed heads and muttered, "How was it done?

"Why are they completely identical?

"If they're planted in soil, will more of them grow?"

The eyes of the four heads held by Reinette Tinekerr turned in different directions before all settling on Frank Lee's face.

Suddenly, the soil stored inside all kinds of vessels within the room flew out and piled before Frank.

Then Frank floated into the air, tumbling with a shocked expression. He was thrown straight into the pile of soil headfirst.

His dangling legs thrashed, but he was unable to pull himself out of the soil pile anytime soon.

Only then did two of Reinette Tinekerr's four heads stretch forward and bite down separately on the letter and the gold coin.

Only after she completely disappeared did Frank Lee finally find the leverage to wrench himself free of the soil and fall to the floor.

She's powerful... Frank sighed with lingering fear. Then he wiped the soil beside his mouth, bit into it, and chewed on it carefully before muttering to himself, "It's a bit sour..."

At that moment, Cattleya, who had just finished a sacrifice in her cabin, sensed something. Her dark purple eyes instinctively turned toward Frank Lee's room, and she vaguely saw a crudely made illusory doll.

The doll didn't have a head!

The scene flashed past, and Cattleya immediately closed her eyes. They burned, and tears streamed uncontrollably down her cheeks.

Her brows gradually knitted together as she muttered in disbelief, "Ancient Bane?"

After sending the Meteorite Crystal and Black-hunting Giant Lizard's spinal fluid to Miss Magician and Miss Justice, Klein returned to the real world. He lay down in a reclining chair and let it rock gently. He began considering where he would head to next.

With news of Gehrman Sparrow's sighting and the matter of Crazy Captain Connors Viktor, it's unlikely that the pirates will openly appear in Bayam for some time. They've either left the harbors or gone into hiding, making it difficult for others to find them.

In other words, there's no need for me to stay here. I can direct matters concerning the Resistance by responding as the Sea God or relaying instructions through Danitz.

Hmm, I'll head to the Seaweed Bar later. I'll get a fake identity, buy a scalped ticket, and head for Desi Bay's Conant City... That's not only the biggest harbor around here, but it's also Davy Raymond's hometown. I previously promised this Red Glove, when I released him from Creeping Hunger, that I would pay this beautiful bay city a visit and tell his daughter that revenge has been exacted. Yes, I'll also think of a way to return the Nightmare's Beyonder characteristic to the Church.

Heh heh, how hypocritical can people be? I'm planning on returning a Nightmare characteristic while planning to steal a Sealed Artifact from behind Saint Samuel Cathedral's Chanis Gate...

Shaking his head, Klein closed his eyes and slept to recover his Spirituality.

After an unknown period of time, he sensed something and opened his eyes. He quickly activated his Spirit Vision.

Then, he saw Reinette Tinekerr walk out of the void.

This messenger was wearing the same complicated black dress, with a letter clasped in one head's teeth.

Who mailed it? Danitz, Vice Admiral Iceberg, Frank, or Anderson?

Klein accepted the letter and nodded gratefully.

"Thank you."

He was always courteous to his powerful messenger of mysterious origins. He didn't wish to be strangled to death one day.

"Do you..." "Want to..." "Immediately..."

Reinette Tinekerr's four heads spoke one after another.

"Reply..."

Klein tore open the envelope, removed the letter, unfolded it, and began to read. Its contents alarmed him as he nearly forgot to reply to her. As for Reinette Tinekerr, she wasn't quick-tempered. She waited silently beside him.

One day, Frank Lee will destroy the world. He definitely needs to be controlled. I can't give him a chance to advance! Seriously, just how obsessed is this fellow with crossbreeding and creating all kinds of strange plants? Eh... the City of Silver is in need of food... A bold idea flashed through Klein's mind.

It was to direct Frank's research efforts towards varieties of food suitable for the City of Silver!

This way, cows, fish, mushrooms, Rose Bishops, the sea, and the world would be safe!

Klein hurriedly looked up and said to his messenger, "Yes, I'll immediately reply."

He immediately rose from the reclining chair, walked to the desk, took out pen and paper, and started writing quickly.

"I have a question. If you eat a mushroom variety you've created, then eat cooked fish and drink a cup of water, will it continue to reproduce?"

After writing this warning, Klein proceeded to the main point.

"Is it possible for you to create wheat that can grow without sunlight, or cows that can produce milk and meat simply by consuming monsters? That seems rather interesting!"

He continued along these lines and wrote a few paragraphs before folding the letter. He then handed it to Reinette Tinekerr and said as naturally as possible, "The postage will be paid by Frank."

"Let's hope..." "He..." "Isn't..." "Dead..." After the messenger's four heads said those words one after another, one of them bit down on the envelope.

Let's hope he isn't dead? Klein jumped in fright. Just as he was about to clarify the matter, Reinette Tinekerr had already entered the spirit world and disappeared.

After contemplating for two seconds, Klein wrote a divination statement and used his topaz pendant to confirm that Frank Lee was still alive.

He silently heaved a sigh of relief, packed up the dried mushrooms, and rubbed his temples before lying back in the reclining chair.

After dinner, at the Seaweed Bar.

Wearing an ordinary-looking face, Klein returned once more.

Unlike before, the customers in the bar were mainly mixed-bloods or natives who had darker skin and curly black hair. They were either part of the gangs in Bayam, or they were secretly working for the Resistance, or even both. There weren't many ordinary people, and the pirates from various countries who commonly frequented the place were all gone. Only a few people dressed as adventurers were drinking liquor and discussing rumors at sea.

Klein swept his gaze across the bar and found Deniel, who Danitz had mentioned. This thin local could provide him with fake identification documents and scalped ship tickets.

Without hesitation, he walked over.

"A second-class ticket to Conant for tomorrow, and an identification document."

Deniel looked up and glanced at him. After some thought, he said, "A total of 20 pounds."

Just a second-class ticket to Conant costs around nine pounds... However, scalped tickets are more expensive to begin with. Together with the forged identification documents, 20 pounds isn't too outrageous... Klein silently did the math before saying, "When can I get it?"

"In 45 minutes," Deniel replied like clockwork. "You can pay 5 pounds first, and then pay the rest after receiving the ticket and identification."

"Alright." Klein didn't haggle as he took out his wallet and pulled out five one-pound notes.

He wasn't worried that anyone would target his wallet, for it might mean that he would save the 20 pounds, or even get more.

Moments after Deniel checked the authenticity of the notes and was about to tell his subordinates to get to work, he suddenly realized that the bar was extremely silent!

Klein sensed it as well and instinctively looked toward the door.

There were two people there. One was wearing a formal tailcoat and a black trench coat, with brown hair neatly combed back. His eyes weren't big, but they were bright and piercing. He had a thin mustache around his mouth, giving him the air of a gentleman despite his slightly disheveled appearance. The other person wore an unusually styled hooded robe. His face was hidden in the shadows, making it impossible to identify him.

The sloppy gentleman scanned the area and was rather pleased with the crowd's reaction. A silver coin tumbled between his fingers as he walked towards Deniel. The hooded person followed behind him, taking something from his clothing and stuffing it into his mouth, producing crunching sounds.

The silver coin stopped moving when the sloppy gentleman came in front of Deniel. He said with a chuckle, "Prepare ten tickets to Pritz Harbor tomorrow for me. They need to be split between three different ships."

"Yes, Mr. Oder." Deniel hurriedly stood to reply.

Klein momentarily failed to recall who the sloppy gentleman tumbling the silver coin was. Only when he heard the name "Oder" did he find the man familiar.

As he was recalling, he saw the man in the hood take out a coffee-colored sweet before popping it into his mouth. He munched on it with a crunch.

After receiving an affirmative reply, Oder and the man didn't stay any longer. Amid the silence, they walked to the staircase and headed for the second floor of the bar.

Deniel exhaled as he turned his head to see Klein looking puzzled. He then said, "Oder. The adventurer, Oder, who serves the Dawn."

...I remember, Silver Coin Viper Oder! He's always claimed that he works for Queen Mystic, but no one could prove it. I can ask Ma'am Hermit later... The last I heard of him was at Damir Harbor. He was apparently mixing with Admiral of Blood's intelligence officer, Old Quinn... However, the latter has already been finished off by Mr. Hanged Man... Klein instantly recalled many things as he deliberated and asked, "What about the other one?"

"Who knows?" Deniel turned his head to instruct his subordinates as he got them to prepare some identification documents and the corresponding ship tickets.

Chapter 722: An Unpeaceful Night

As Silver Coin Viper Oder wasn't a pirate, there were all kinds of rumors about him, most of which could hardly be verified. Klein retracted his gaze from the staircase and walked to the bar counter. He found a seat at the counter and tapped it.

"A cup of Zarhar."

This was a locally produced malt beer. It was a lot cheaper than Southville beer that needed to be shipped over from the Northern Continent.

"3 pence." The bartender recovered from his silent state as he picked up an overturned cup.

The crowd in the bar began to break out into whispers as they were illuminated by the gas wall lamps. All of them were discussing the reason for Silver Coin Viper Oder's purchase of ten tickets.

"He's definitely being pursued by someone. Ten tickets among three ships... It's clearly to prevent his pursuers from knowing which ship they boarded!" A gang member with his sleeves rolled up, revealing his tattoo, shared his view based on his experience from evading capture twice.

An adventurer drinking Lanti Proof scoffed.

"You don't understand Oder. If his plan was that simple, he wouldn't have the title 'Silver Coin Viper.'

"I dare bet that he won't be on any of those liners from those ten tickets!

"The only thing I can confirm is that they're heading to Pritz Harbor."

Another adventurer shook his head when he heard that.

"Perhaps the news of him heading to Pritz Harbor is fake as well."

The gang member from before was taken aback by what he heard. Refusing to be one-upped, he said, "According to your descriptions, Oder has likely thought of what you figured out. That's precisely why he's heading to Pritz Harbor and will be on one of the three ships!"

The two adventurers were about to retort him, but they decided that there was quite a nonzero chance of that happening after some careful thought. Momentarily, none of them said a word.

This made the gang member extremely happy as he downed the rest of his liquor.

Klein was holding a cup of Zarhar as he sipped it while listening to the conversation. He was waiting for the fake identification and tickets he needed.

There's another 45 minutes. I hope nothing happens. Don't make the bar into a mess... He silently prayed as he drew the crimson moon inwardly.

The light-yellow beer slowly dropped in volume while Klein glanced from time to time at the wall clock or the entrance, hoping that time would pass faster.

Half an hour later, the door to the bar suddenly flew open with a loud thud as the evening wind gushed in.

No way... The corners of Klein's lips twitched as he held back his urge to smile wryly. He turned toward the sound.

Standing by the door were five people. Their leader had black hair and brown eyes, with deep-set features and a sharply defined face. He looked Loenese and looked to be in his early forties.

His expression was cold and he exuded a natural air of dominance. It made everyone in the bar fall silent without realizing it.

And the three men and the woman behind him were in trench coats. They didn't hide the fact that they were holding revolvers, and that they would instantly aim and shoot at the slightest sign of trouble.

I don't know them. They aren't on any wanted lists, nor do they have bounties on their heads... Klein mumbled to himself as he maintained his Spectator state.

The five intruders suddenly scattered as they came before different customers, bent their backs slightly, and looked at them before asking, "Where is Silver Coin Viper Oder?"

The customers were hesitant to give an answer when they saw the black muzzle pointed at them, along with the handle made of ivory and ebony which exuded a strange sense of beauty under the lights.

"T-they went to the second floor!" The customers who had been asked nearly pointed to the staircase in unison.

Someone is really pursuing Oder. This is an act against Queen Mystic, or has Silver Coin Viper done something himself? Or could it be because of the mysterious hooded man beside him who was eating sweets? Klein drank another mouthful of beer as he saw the intruders send four people up to the second floor. One was left behind to continue questioning the customers.

Soon, the latter grasped the situation of Oder's request to purchase tickets from Deniel. Immediately, the person walked straight to the thin and dark-skinned black marketer, and he asked in a heavy voice, "Tell me honestly. Where is Oder heading with those tickets?"

Deniel didn't put on airs despite his connections. He forced a smile and said, "He didn't make it clear. He requested ten tickets that are to be distributed across three different ships. The departure date is set for tomorrow with the destination being Pritz Harbor."

"For real?" The questioner was a fierce-looking man in his twenties.

Deniel softly replied, "You can ask anyone here. All of them heard it."

"Dogsh*t!" The man shoved Deniel angrily as he turned to walk towards the other customers.

Deniel staggered backward and was about to fall and strike his head against the edge of a tiny circular table when he suddenly felt a force steady his shoulder. Instantly, he regained his balance.

He subconsciously looked over and saw that it was the customer who had just requested to purchase a fake identity and scalped tickets.

"Thank you." Deniel then muttered through clenched teeth, "Those damned military hyenas!"

The person who had helped him was Klein. He didn't wish for anything to happen to this "ticket scalper"; after all, he had paid a deposit of 5 pounds.

Of course, helping the innocent was also a habit of his.

Military hyenas? In Bayam, this description often refers to people from MI9... What did Silver Coin Viper Oder do? Klein silently asked himself as he eliminated the possibility that someone was targeting Queen Mystic.

To the Loen military, it was meaningless.

As he was thinking, the MI9 members who had headed up to the second floor rushed down. As they ran, they said to their partner, "He has long fled through the window!"

The group of people came and went in a rush. Soon, the bar resumed its usual din, but the still gently wobbling main door proved that the earlier disturbance had been real.

Klein's wait for his fake identification documents and scalped ticket paid off. He didn't need to worry about any possible interruptions.

After paying the remaining 15 pounds, he left the Seaweed Bar and returned to the ordinary inn he stayed at.

John Yode... This name is way too simple, isn't it? Before I return to Backlund, I need to make a more realistic identification document. Klein flipped through the series of identification documents before throwing them inside his suitcase.

He took a bath and relaxed, preparing to leave Bayam the next day and begin the last stage of his sea "travels."

At this moment, he heard knocking at the door.

Who is it? Klein hurriedly took off his bathrobe, dressed, and walked to the door.

Outside were a few policemen in black. One of them looked Loenese, while the rest were either mixed-bloods or pure natives.

"What's the matter?" Klein asked, puzzled.

"Please show us some identification," a mixed-blood said politely since the gentleman in front of him was apparently a Loenese.

Thankfully, I just made one. Otherwise, I'll be spending the night at the police station, or I'd have to flee on the spot, change my looks, and redo everything... Klein mumbled as he returned to his room, and took out the identification documents.

The Loen police officer casually flipped through it as he said, "Mr. Yode, are you living alone?"

"Yes, everyone in the inn can vouch for me," Klein replied frankly.

The Loenese police officer revealed a smile and said, "Have you seen this person before?"

As he spoke, a constable beside him unfolded a portrait. On it was an elder who was abnormally thin with messy white hair. Apart from that, nothing stood out.

"No." Klein shook his head.

"He likes to eat sweets," the Loenese police officer added.

"Sweets..." Klein suddenly recalled the mysterious hooded man behind Silver Coin Viper Oder. He had been eating plenty of coffee-colored sweets in a short period of time.

After a short deliberation, Klein said without hiding anything, "Perhaps. Back when I was in the Seaweed Bar, I saw a man who loved eating sweets and was following Silver Coin Viper Oder."

The Loenese officer didn't hide his disappointment. After a simple word of thanks, he ended the questioning.

Only after the officer began knocking on the other guests' doors did Klein close the wooden door and return to the reclining chair.

Oder's matter has not only attracted MI9, but also prompted the governor-general's office to send personnel on a citywide search. This is quite something... he mused and decided to head above the gray fog to browse the prayer points of light around the Sea God Scepter. He could gain more information from the prayers of the believers in Bayam. He didn't wish to become embroiled in a massive maelstrom because of an ill-judged response.

After entering the bathroom, he went above the gray fog where he summoned the white bone scepter from the junk pile. Swirling around it were countless points of light.
